# Decisions and assumptions

Engineering and business assumptions for the delivery. Each entry records who decided it, why, and what would reopen it. Architecture decisions for the product itself live in `docs/architecture/adr/` (from P1) and are linked here.

The sets differ only in the slipped predecessor's milestone.<br>- Nothing is stored or overwritten except the slip's own `affected` evidence (ADR-0044 §7). A seventh gate or an eleventh dimension is not creatable (REQ-S02-001, S06-006).<br>**Q5** (which text is verbatim): the playbook text (B0134–B0141, B0150, B0152) is rendered verbatim. It is the authoritative methodology (CLAUDE.md 'Sources'); the master prompt is treated as a paraphrase.<br>**Q6** (90-day day numbering): adopted. Day *d* = start date + *d* calendar days, as REQ-PB-086's acceptance fixes (start 2026-10-01 → Days 31–60 fall 2026-11-01…2026-11-30). A task that falls on a non-working day shows a working-day warning and is never silently shifted.<br>**Q7** (demo isolation): adopted. A separately initialized demo database selected by configuration; the `seed-demo`/`reset-demo` CLI is refused when `MTH_ENV=production`. Any compose-profile change is requested from devops through the orchestrator, additively.<br>**Q8** (export libraries and fonts): not blocking. The npm registry is reachable from the orchestrator's environment through the session proxy (checked 2026-10-13: HTTP 200; `environment.md` 'npm registry access'). Agent runs stay offline. ARCH-04 chooses the libraries and an OFL-licensed Arabic and Latin font, with official documentation, support-window and licence evidence. The orchestrator then installs them into the lockfile on request (p5-plan §5.3), before BE-M/BE-N. If a chosen package cannot be provisioned, the affected checks are BLOCKED and the user is told.<br>**Q9** (what a migration re-approval does to an approved gate): adopted as recommended. The approved G2 decision and its snapshot stay in history, the gate is marked 're-approval required', and a G2 submission task is created. Later-phase work continues, but the next gate cannot be submitted until the re-approval. This is a product default, recorded in ADR-0040 as configurable business policy, not a business approval granted by an agent.<br>**Q10** (restatement authority): adopted. The approver of the KPI change request holds `kpi_restatement.approve`, and the requester cannot approve (SoD default).<br>**Q11** (quiet hours and digests): adopted. In P5 the digest is one in-app digest notification per user per window. A new migration redefines `p3_instantiate_transformation` to read the pinned scoring-defaults version and adds `approval_basis = 'studio_default'`. Its output stays byte-identical to DG3 while the published defaults equal the source (B0076); a regression test proves that. The domain reviewer passed for the seventh round in a row, and qa raised nothing new. code-security raised two Low findings on the media-type refusal path: F-DG2-350, a BE14 regression where an unmatched route sent octet-stream got 400 instead of 404; and F-DG2-351, which predates BE14, where HTAB before `;` passed the essence check but matched no Fastify parser, so the refusal named application/json even on the octet-stream-only upload.<br>(1) **Interrupted run (D-059 precedent).** The first T-DG2-BE15 run was killed by a container restart at about 21:59Z, mid-verification, without `meta.json` or a handback. Its working-tree implementation was committed unverified as WIP `08a4f63`. Its transcript and partial logs were moved to `docs/delivery/test-evidence/DG2/be15-orphaned/` for provenance only; they are not cited as evidence. T-DG2-BE15B reviewed the WIP critically, completed it and re-ran the full verification from scratch (`e974d78`).<br>(2) **The fix.** `parseContentType` parses the header per RFC 9110 §8.3.1: OWS (SP/HTAB) is allowed around `;`, the essence is the lower-cased type/subtype, and parameters are tokens or quoted-strings. A request is accepted only if its essence is in the route's `consumes`. An accepted header is rewritten to the canonical `essence[; name=value]` form before Fastify's parser lookup, which reads it after `preParsing`, so the check and the lookup cannot disagree. Every refusal names the route's declared set, including the `FST_ERR_CTP_INVALID_MEDIA_TYPE` backstop and `restrictParserTo`. An unmatched route never has its body parsed or refused: it answers 404 `not_found` for every media type and framing, with `Connection: close`, and the body is never drained. The cost is that a client pipelining behind that 404 must reconnect.<br>(3) **Deliberate tightenings**, each 200/201 before and none sent by the web client: malformed parameters are refused; duplicate Content-Type lines are refused, because Node keeps only the first and an intermediary may honour the last (RFC 9110 §5.3); and a JSON `charset` other than UTF-8 (including `utf8`) is refused. Per RFC 8259 §8.1 JSON between systems MUST be UTF-8, and the API decodes JSON as strict UTF-8 whatever the header says, so accepting another charset would mean the header and the decoder silently disagree.<br>Every fix has a negative control against `3b2ab15`. The orchestrator re-verified the integrated tree in both locale settings. All 32 steps exited 0: unit 765/765 four times per setting (Node 22 and 24), integration 570/570, e2e 60 (chromium-en and chromium-ar), openapi 161 operations, and `validate --register DG2`, `--historical --stage DG1` and `--pipeline`. | `docs/delivery/handbacks/DG2/T-DG2-BE15-backend-workflow-engineer.md`; `apps/api/src/modules/platform/media-types.ts`; `docs/delivery/test-evidence/DG2/be15-orphaned/` | Reopen if the central decision and the parser lookup disagree on any Content-Type spelling, a refusal names a media type the operation does not declare, an unmatched route answers anything but 404, or a legitimate client needs a non-UTF-8 JSON charset. |

| D-055 | 2026-10-01 | delivery-orchestrator | **DG1 round-9 (F-DG1-129/213/010, all Low/non-mandatory): flip the module lint's Node-built-in and `process.*` checks from DENYLISTS to DEFAULT-DENY ALLOW-LISTS — superseding the "closed as found / exhaustive for the pinned Node" framing of D-053/D-054.** Rounds 4–8 closed loader/exec routes one at a time (`eval`/`constructor`/`Reflect` F-DG1-124; `node:process` F-DG1-125; `node:sqlite` F-DG1-127; `node:test`/`process.execve` F-DG1-128), and round 8 still surfaced `process.kill(pid,'SIGUSR1')` / `process._debugProcess(pid)` (start the in-process V8 inspector — the capability of the banned `node:inspector` — F-DG1-129 code-security and F-DG1-213 qa, the same route found independently), while F-DG1-010 showed the "exhaustive for the pinned Node 22" claim named the wrong runtime (production is Node 24; the sweep ran on Node 22). A denylist over Node's evolving surface cannot converge or be version-independent. **Decision:** `apps/api/src/architecture.testkit.ts` now **allow-lists** the built-ins and process members module source legitimately uses and **denies everything else**: `SAFE_NODE_BUILTINS = {crypto, fs, fs/promises, os, path, url, util}` (bareAllowed: a `node:` specifier is allowed only if in this set) and `SAFE_PROCESS_MEMBERS = {env, exit, argv, once}` (rule 3: `process.<member>` is a violation unless in this set). Every other built-in (module, vm, worker_threads, inspector, repl, child_process, cluster, process, sqlite, test, wasi, v8, net, http, …) and every other process member (kill, _kill, _debugProcess, execve, binding, _linkedBinding, dlopen, getBuiltinModule, abort, …) — **and anything a later Node version adds** — is denied by default. The check is therefore **version-independent** (fixes F-DG1-010's root cause: no per-Node enumeration/sweep) and structurally closes the module-IMPORT and process-MEMBER loader/exec classes version-independently (fixes F-DG1-129/213). It does NOT by itself cover a native-loader MEMBER of an allow-listed module (F-DG1-130, `crypto.setEngine`, name-banned by rule 1) nor, by itself, the runtime-enumeration reach of such a member (F-DG1-132/215) — that route is closed by rule 5 (named-imports-only for node:crypto, F-DG1-133); default-deny is not a claim that no further adjacent route exists. The allow-lists were validated against the real module tree (`moduleViolations` zero): module source imports four of the seven built-ins (`crypto`, `fs`, `path`, `url`) and uses none of the four process members — the remaining entries (`fs/promises`, `os`, `util`; and `env`, `exit`, `argv`, `once`) are **reviewed safe headroom** (read/utility built-ins and read/lifecycle process members a module commonly needs), each confirmed to carry no code-loading, exec, native or debug capability, NOT claimed as current usage (F-DG1-131; a conservative allow-list, kept small). Residuals shrink to the genuinely irreducible: (a) runtime data-flow; (b) runtime code-generation then `import()` of a literal same-module path via an allowed built-in such as `node:fs` (a module may legitimately read files; a write-API-only ban is brittle); (c) `WebAssembly` global instantiation (a wasm exec, not a JS-module loader). The default-deny decides which built-in MODULES a module may import; the MEMBERS of an allow-listed module are covered by a recorded member-audit in the testkit header (F-DG1-130): the only member of the seven that loads/executes/natively-loads code is `crypto.setEngine` (a native `dlopen`), now banned by rule 1 (every spelled form) and, since rule 5 (F-DG1-132/133), unreachable by namespace enumeration — a module may import node:crypto through named imports only, so its namespace/default binding never becomes an enumerable value (the former Object.entries(c)/Map route is a violation at the import). The audit was performed on Node v22.22.2 (the build-sandbox floor) and re-run by the orchestrator on Node v24.21.0 (the production target, ADR-0001) in round 11 with the identical result — `crypto.setEngine` the only native-loader member, all dangerous `process.*` members denied by default-deny, and the static lint behaving identically (it parses the AST, independent of the Node runtime); evidence `docs/delivery/test-evidence/DG1/orchestrator/node24-member-audit.log`. It is re-run when the Node floor/target changes. The lint remains static defence-in-depth for the ADR-0002 module boundaries over human-reviewed code in a read-only production source tree — not a runtime security boundary. | DG1 round 8: all three reviewers PASS on candidate `sha256:e27eaf5f…`; the open items (F-DG1-010/129/213) were Low/non-mandatory and all pointed at denylist non-convergence. **Evidence:** `architecture.test.ts` 101/101 incl. default-deny positive/negative controls + the real-module-tree check (`moduleViolations` zero); the full unit suite 294/294 (no legit module import/member newly flagged); an orchestrator probe confirming process.kill/_debugProcess/getBuiltinModule, node:inspector/net/http denied and node:crypto/fs/path/url/util/os + process.env/exit/argv/once allowed; `@mth/api` typecheck/lint/prettier clean; the round-9 freeze + its three PASS reviews + auditor. | A legitimate module needs a built-in or `process` member not on an allow-list (then it is reviewed and added deliberately), or the project adopts an even stricter default-deny for the Node globals (WebAssembly, fetch). |

| D-018 | 2026-09-28 | delivery-orchestrator | **Findings integrity** (F-DG0-101, F-DG0-110). That entry must be PASS, by a reviewer who is not the owner, and bound to that reviewer's own post-freeze run for the round. `findings.json` must mirror it exactly. The fix revision must be a commit contained in both the verified round's candidate and the gate candidate, and the verification evidence must be existing repository files. Every review round recorded in `stages.json` must still exist, every round directory must be recorded, and no file under `reviews/<DGx>/` or `runs/<DGx>/` may ever be deleted from git history. Every finding raised in any review round (the reviewer sidecars under `reviews/<DGx>/round-N/`) must exist in `findings.json`. The fields the reviewer sets (stage, requirement, severity, mandatory flag, title, reporter) can't be altered there. Findings can't be relabelled to another stage. Only Low, non-mandatory findings can be accepted as observations, and that needs a specialist reviewer and the auditor. | This stops unresolved findings escaping a gate by being dropped, relabelled or downgraded. | — |
| D-019 | 2026-09-28 | delivery-orchestrator | **Classifier-outage resume.** When an agent run stops only because the permission classifier returned no verdict repeatedly, the runner resumes the **same session** (same invocation reference) after a pause, up to 6 times, and records `classifier_outage_resumes` in meta. | D-020 | 2026-09-28 | delivery-orchestrator | **Write-guard hardening** (F-DG0-105). | D-021 | 2026-09-28 | delivery-orchestrator | **Reviewer artefacts are bound to their run and are write-once** (F-DG0-115, F-DG0-208, F-DG0-117, F-DG0-102).<br>• **Run outputs.** The runner snapshots the tree before and after each run. It records in `meta.json`:<br>• `outputs`: every file the run window changed, path → SHA-256;<br>• `written_by_tools`: the paths the agent targeted with file tools;<br>• `tool_authored`: the paths whose final bytes equal a replay of the agent's own **successful** Write/Edit/MultiEdit calls from the transcript (F-DG0-119).<br>It auto-commits the run directory plus the `tool_authored` files, using literal NUL-separated pathspecs, and fails with exit 70 if the commit fails (F-DG0-120). It auto-commits a reviewer's run directory together with its written files immediately.<br>• **Output binding.** The validator requires every gate review record, and every record and sidecar used to close or accept a finding, to equal what its run wrote.<br>• **Write-once history.** For every path under `reviews/<DGx>/`, `runs/<DGx>/`, `candidates/<DGx>/` and `assignments/<DGx>/round-*/`, all commit events against every parent (merges included) must introduce **one and the same blob**, with no modify, type-change or delete events. HEAD and the bytes on disk (hashed directly, so skip-worktree can't hide edits) must equal that blob. No commit may be back-dated before one of its parents. This is a root-cause redesign: F-DG0-115/118 showed that diff inspection misses merges and index flags, and the round-6 residual showed that a "first added" rule can be defeated by forged commit dates. The validator also replays each bound run's hash-bound transcript itself rather than trusting `meta.tool_authored`.<br>• **Per-freeze manifests.** Each freeze writes its own write-once manifest `candidates/<DGx>/<id-prefix>.manifest.json`, and every review round's `candidate_id`, `frozen_at` and `source_commit` must match it. The manifest must also recompute from its `source_commit`, and a bound run must have started from a commit that already contains it (F-DG0-212).<br>• **Sidecar-only acceptance.** Observations are accepted only through bound sidecar entries (`status_after: ACCEPTED_OBSERVATION`) from a specialist reviewer and from the release-auditor. | Reviewer-written evidence can no longer be edited in place undetected. Closure and acceptance need artefacts produced by the reviewer's own run. Evidence from rounds 1–3 predates output recording, so every finding closure is re-confirmed in round 4 by bound runs. **Residual (disclosed):** run evidence is self-hashed rather than externally attested. A party able to fabricate a complete, internally consistent run directory *and* commit it as new history can still forge evidence. The remaining controls are the auditor's transcript review and the published git history. | An external attestation service becomes available, such as signed transcripts. |
| D-022 | 2026-09-28 | delivery-orchestrator | **Prompt binding through CLI replay.** The runner sends each prompt, including resume prompts, as a stream-json user message with `--input-format stream-json --replay-user-messages`. The validator accepts a run only if its transcript holds such a replayed message whose text starts with the runner prompt for that role and stage, and which carries the run's `run_id` and `session_id`. The same words inside a tool result don't count. | **Self-found defect, disclosed.** The prompt check added in the round-1 repair (F-DG0-102) looked for any `user` event containing the prompt text. `claude -p` does not echo its argument prompt, so on real transcripts the check matched nothing, or matched tool-result text where an agent had read `run-agent.sh`. It passed only on synthetic test fixtures. The orchestrator found this while pre-checking closures before round 7. **Consequence:** none of the DG0 runs before this change carry a replayed prompt, so none of them can bind a closure. In round 7, every reviewer re-confirms every finding it has raised. Verified end-to-end with the real CLI in a disposable clone, for both a first run and a resume. **Round-7 hardening:** every transcript check is unconditional, so an empty or newline-only transcript fails them all (F-DG0-132). The assignment path and SHA-256 named in the replayed prompt must equal `meta.assignment` / `meta.assignment_sha256` (F-DG0-133). | — |

The guard protects the repository plus every worktree that `git worktree list` reports, scoping each path from the deepest containing root. Writes outside all guarded roots are scratch and are allowed. **Also fixed** (F-DG0-224): the validator resolves its own directory with `fileURLToPath`, so paths with spaces or non-ASCII characters work. | Before this change, the hook chose which guard to run with `git rev-parse` from its current directory. The home directory is never scratch, even when it lies inside the temp directory, so `~/.claude`, `~/.gitconfig`, `/etc` and shell rc files are blocked.<br>• The runner starts agents with `--setting-sources project,local` (superseded by D-025: `project` only), so user-level settings (for example `disableAllHooks`) can't switch the guard off. | D-025 | 2026-09-28 | delivery-orchestrator | **Agent shells run in an OS sandbox.** Root-cause response to F-DG0-137, F-DG0-226 and the recurring findings that the agent shell was unguarded. The runner generates each run's settings with `tools/agents/agent_settings.py`: the role's guard hook plus a Claude Code Bash sandbox (bubblewrap) with `enabled`, `failIfUnavailable` and `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`. Its `denyWrite` list covers `.git`, `.claude`, `.mcp.json`, `CLAUDE*.md`, `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`, `.github`, `tools/{gates,agents,source}`, `docs/source`, `trading_agent` and the delivery records, in the repository and every worktree. The runner's config scan now covers Claude configuration and ignore files anywhere in the repository and worktrees, ignored or not, plus symlinked hooks. This was corrected by F-DG0-231; the first probe wrote under `/tmp/claude-0`, which is `$TMPDIR`;<br>• `denyWrite` wins over `allowWrite`, and can name paths that don't exist yet;<br>• denying the whole working directory breaks sandbox initialisation;<br>• `enableWeakerNestedSandbox` is required, because the container has no unprivileged user namespaces;<br>• sandboxed commands have no network. | D-026 | 2026-09-28 | delivery-orchestrator | **The orchestrator never executes agent-writable code, or imports from agent-writable paths, outside a sandbox** (F-DG0-140, F-DG0-229). After resolving its inputs the runner changes to `/`, and only the `claude` process itself runs in the agent's working directory. Node helpers import only `node:` built-ins and relative files inside `tools/`, which agents can't write.<br>• **Pre-freeze:** `tools/gates/prefreeze.sh` runs every check that executes candidate code (extraction, tests, register and pipeline checks, `docs/analysis/tools/*.py`) through the new `tools/gates/sandbox-run.sh`. No other process can see or change the work clone.<br>• **Orchestrator practice:** the orchestrator keeps its helper scripts and control files out of `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR`, which are agent-writable, and uses a root-only directory under its home instead. It was latent because `checkWriteOnce` runs at gate/audit time, and DG0 has not reached a gate.<br>• **Root cause.** Round-17 qa hit the account session limit before writing its verdict record, leaving record-less sidecars. `import-findings.mjs` used to **throw** when a verifications sidecar had no backing record, and the round-17 remediation "removed the unbindable sidecars" — a delete of committed review evidence, exactly what write-once forbids.<br>• **Hardening (done, this commit).** `import-findings.mjs` now **skips** an interrupted run's record-less verifications with a logged warning instead of throwing: the findings they targeted stay pending and are re-verified in a later round, and the sidecars are left in place as honest evidence. Verified after the rewrite: `checkWriteOnce` = **0 errors**, the two files' blobs are byte-identical to the originals, the validator suite is 47/47, and the D-033 work is intact. The `refs/original/` backup was removed and the branch force-pushed (`--force-with-lease`) to HEAD `5a49719`; a local backup ref `backup/pre-writeonce-repair-565ab3f` is kept. F-DG0-152/153/239 are now marked FIXED at the post-rewrite SHAs `1a99d13` (152/153) and `38ffd1d` (239). | D-033 | 2026-09-29 | delivery-orchestrator | **Each run enters its own scope-only Landlock domain, so one run cannot reach or signal another through the shared host `/proc`** (F-DG0-152, High, mandatory).<br>• **The finding.** D-030 keeps the host PID namespace shared and binds a read-write host `/proc` (a reviewer's nested bubblewrap needs a "fully visible" procfs to mount its own fresh one). Because concurrent runs (§0.2 / D-028) execute under the same uid 0, `ptrace_may_access(PTRACE_MODE_READ)` between them passes, so run A could traverse `/proc/<B-pid>/root/…` and `/proc/<B-pid>/cwd/…` into run B's read-write areas (its evidence directory and its private `TMPDIR`) and could send run B signals — a cross-run integrity break the read-only binds and capability drop do not cover, since it goes through the peer's own mounts. Demonstrated in round 18 by code-security.<br>• **The fix.** Just after the capabilities are pinned (`setpriv`) and before `claude` execs, `run-agent.sh` runs the whole agent through `tools/agents/landlock_exec.py`, which puts the process in a fresh Landlock domain (ABI ≥ 6; this kernel reports 7) with a **scope-only** ruleset: `handled_access_fs = 0`, `handled_access_net = 0`, `scoped = LANDLOCK_SCOPE_SIGNAL | LANDLOCK_SCOPE_ABSTRACT_UNIX_SOCKET`. Two properties follow. (1) Being in **any** non-empty Landlock domain restricts ptrace to that domain and its descendants; every run enters its **own** domain, so two runs are **siblings** and the kernel refuses each one's ptrace of the other — hence its `/proc/<peer>/root|cwd` access is refused (`Permission denied`). (2) `SIGNAL` scoping refuses a signal to a process outside the domain (cross-run `kill` refused), and `ABSTRACT_UNIX_SOCKET` scoping refuses connecting to an abstract socket owned outside the domain (closing that cross-run channel in the shared network namespace). Because **no** filesystem or network access is handled, the run's own areas stay writable and its nested bubblewrap with a fresh `--proc` still works. The domain is inherited across `execve` and by every descendant, so it covers the CLI, the agent's shell and its nested sandboxes; within one run all processes share the single domain and operate on their own `/proc` and sockets normally.<br>• **Fail-closed.** If Landlock is unavailable (kernel/ABI too old) or any step fails, `landlock_exec.py` raises and `claude` never execs, so a run can never proceed with the cross-run hole silently open (CLAUDE.md: a control that did not run is not "passed").<br>• **Evidence & validation.** `sandbox.json` records `landlock: {scoped: ["SIGNAL","ABSTRACT_UNIX_SOCKET"], handled_access_fs: 0, handled_access_net: 0, per_run_domain: true}` and the validator (`tools/gates/lib/rules.mjs`) now requires it on every gate review/audit record. Test: `tools/agents/tests/process-sandbox.test.mjs` "F-DG0-152: a per-run Landlock domain stops a run reaching or signalling another run via /proc/<peer>/root\|cwd" — a positive control strips the wrapper and shows the cross-run writes and signal succeed (the D-030-only design), then with the wrapper every cross-run write and the signal are refused, the attacker's own areas stay writable, and a nested `bwrap` with a fresh `--proc` still works. Mirrors the reviewer's round-18 mitigation spike (`docs/delivery/test-evidence/DG0/code-security/round-18/{r18-landlock-spike.mjs,04-landlock-spike.log}`).<br>• **Residuals.** This narrows threat-model residual 8 (the shared PID namespace no longer permits cross-run `/proc` access or signals). Residual 7 (a file tool writing a uid-0-writable `/proc/sys` tunable) is unchanged by this decision, because `handled_access_fs = 0` deliberately restricts no filesystem access; F-DG0-152 closes only the **cross-run** amplification. **Correction (D-036, F-DG0-154):** the "own-run only" scope stated here was wrong — `/proc/sys` is host-global kernel state, so such a write affects the whole host; see the corrected threat-model residual 7. It remains a host availability / kernel-tunable risk, not repository or gate-record integrity. | Found in round 18 by code-security (F-DG0-152, High, mandatory), which also spiked the mitigation. **Evidence:** the reviewer's spike and log above; the new process-sandbox test with its positive control (fails on the previous code, which has no per-run domain); the validator test for the required `landlock` evidence; full suites pass; the round-19 pre-freeze and reviews. | A future kernel or environment lacks Landlock ABI ≥ 6: the run fails closed (the agent cannot start) and the gate is BLOCKED until the environment provides it or a new decision re-scopes the control; never worked around by dropping the wrapper. Narrowing the scope set (e.g. dropping `ABSTRACT_UNIX_SOCKET`) needs a new decision and re-review. |
